ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-17-senat-21-1.u6	Mr. Marshal! High Senate! As draftsman of the committee, I could in principle limit myself to presenting only the result of voting on the Foreign Service Act, but... [[Talking in the O.R.]] [[I would ask you to focus on the senator's report. If there is a need to talk, please lead them in clowns.]] That's right, I'm joining that request. Ladies and gentlemen, I am going to describe the proceedings of the committee because we have devoted a great deal of attention to the bill under review, considering that it is a fundamental law for our foreign service and for Poland's position in the world. The committee meeting lasted about three hours. The final result of the vote - 6 votes against, 1 vote against, no abstentions - reflects the discussion at this meeting. I must say that as chairman of the committee, I have tried to allow all the opinions that have been submitted to us. This time we were lucky to have a lot of opinions. There was, of course, a relationship between the Deputy Minister of Foreign Affairs, Minister Jablonski, who, from the government's point of view, presented arguments for this law. In addition to this voice, there was no positive vote on this law, both among those who were formal representatives of the institutions who shared their opinions with us and among the advisers of the Committee on Foreign Affairs and the European Union. The following opinions came to us before the committee meeting. We have received 2 opinions from the Solidarity. The first was the opinion of the NSZZ National Commission, which was very critical of this law, and the second was the opinion of the NSZZ Works Commission, which was even more critical than the first. It can therefore be said that the trade union, which rarely speaks in matters of legislation presented by the government, has subjected the law to strict assessment and very serious criticism. We also had two opinions from the Ombudsman. The first one referred to the idea of introducing the so-called. diplomatic secrecy as another, absent from the existing Act on the protection of classified information, classified griffin for information originating from the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. The Ombudsman's second opinion referred to the entire bill and was also very critical. Finally, we had, ladies and gentlemen, the opinions of the foreign affairs committee's advisers presented in the sitting room or remotely. They were, you can say, crushing for this bill. The chairman of the Works Commission of the NSZZ.Solidarity of Foreign Service Workers, Ambassador Zdzisław Raczyński, who came to the service already in independent Poland, pointed out that the nature of the work on this law was quite different from that of the law, which was adopted in consensus in 2001. I must say that the work on this law lasted for several months and was carried out as should be the work on all other laws, which was a tradition for 25 years of independent Poland, for the first 25 years of independent Poland, listened to opinions of various political backgrounds, suffered these positions and finally came to some consensual solutions. I watched this work as the then Minister of National Defence in the years 1999/2000 and I must say that ultimately that law adopted by the minority government of the Election Action Solidarity was a reflection of the position of the earlier coalition of the Union of Freedom The Action of the Election Solidarity and other groups that were then in opposition. In other words, the work on this law is described by the chairman of the Works Commission, which considered that they were hasty and in breach of the law, as indicated by the analysis of the legal office of the National Commission, which he referred to. I note that the legal office of the National Commission for Solidarity considers that the work on this law was carried out in violation of the law. That's one thing. Secondly, Solvency claims this law to be a misdiagnosis of the present state of affairs and considers that this law will not lead to a hermeticisation of foreign service, because the access routes to foreign service are sufficiently open and the government's assumption that foreign service is currently too hermetic is a misconception. This is what the representative of the "Solidarity" points out in his opinion. And it gives facts, it says that 1/3 ambassadors of the Republic of Poland in the world and almost half of ambassadors in the countries of the European Union come, so to speak, from outside the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. This is very important data to confirm this opinion. Thirdly, in its opinion, Solvency considers that the Act is contrary to the Law on Departments and, in fact, to the philosophy adopted in the Law on Departments. In the case of that law, the government was in favour of the vice-ministers being included in the civil service corps, while the solutions contained in that law go in the opposite direction. In addition, it considers that this law may introduce a deep dysfunction in the foreign service. What does that mean? This means that it can lead to an increase in the number of conflicts, to confusion in the management process, to a lack of clear competences, to overlapping of competences between the main pillars of Polish diplomacy, i.e. Minister of Foreign Affairs, Head of Foreign Service, whose role is extremely exposed in this law, and, finally, Director General of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. So much for headquarters. As far as the institutions are concerned, this law could lead to deep conflicts between the ambassador and his deputy. Ambassador, who, according to this law, would perform a purely political function, to carry out the function of representative of the Republic of Poland towards the country or international organisation in which he is affiliated and his deputy, responsible for all matters relating to current management, finance, staff, etc. This dysfunction of the foreign service is to refer to the opinion presented by the committee The aim is to relieve the ambassador of responsibility in a large area, but to transfer this responsibility to his deputy, which will lead to inevitable conflicts between the ambassador and his deputy, but also between the Director-General of the Ministry and the Head of Foreign Service, in view of the lack of clearly defined competences of these 2 institutions on the ground, in the country. In fact, solidarity speaks more strongly because it speaks of the fact that it is unacceptable to prohibit officials It's another case. For professional diplomats in trade unions, it is incompatible with international regulations, including Convention 151 of the International Labour Organisation, which specifies precisely that such restrictions are It's only possible in uniformed services. It was also a topic discussed during this meeting, that foreign service is not a uniformed formation, it is not considered to be such and should never escape. And that even in some uniform formations like the police, trade unions are allowed. Why, then, should trade unions not exist in the foreign service? At the end of this opinion, "Solidarity..." I would like to introduce it to you very carefully, because not everyone has had the opportunity to get to know it. It refers to the fact that the provisions of this law are, first, contrary to the Constitution, second, incompatible with international law and thirdly incompatible with the legislation in force in Poland. We were asking in very detail why these provisions would be contrary to the Constitution, as at that time we had not yet had the opinion which was prepared for the purpose of the committee, for the purposes of the entire Senate by a constitutionalist, Prof. Chmaj, who has shown this in his expert opinion. And Solidarity put forward this view. In its view, the non-constitutional restrictions related to the restriction of union freedom, freedom of expression, the possibility for foreign servants to speak publicly. The Bureau of the national committee received the draft law for an opinion very late. This is another argument showing that this law not only in the opinion of Members... but that the work on this law was not in accordance with any legislative technique rules, because the bureau of the National Commission of the NSZZ (Solidarity) received a draft law for an opinion from the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, as the President of the Council said, about 11.00 22 January, i.e. a dozen hours after the act was passed. Several hours after the bill was passed in the Sejm. Finally, of course, there was a thread of ex-lege retiring all diplomats who were over sixty-five years old. There is, however, a gateway for a foreign minister who could accept an appeal against such a decision and leave in the service of such an appealing diplomat, but the Solidarity clearly states that, firstly, this principle is incompatible with the law in force in Poland, and secondly, this possibility without specifying in the law the criteria for which the Foreign Minister could invoke, allows such great voluntaryism that practically makes the fate of all these diplomats dependent on the recognition of the Foreign Minister. So not the procedure, not the terms, not the criteria, but the foreign minister would decide that. So much for "Solidarity." We have also heard the opinions of the Senate Legislative Office. It was a very broad opinion. I'm just gonna talk about the most important things, i.e. this provision, which causes work in the foreign service to expire on the date on which the person concerned reaches the age of sixty-five, i.e. the article. 3 (1) (a) 1 point 1 of the Act. The Legal Office states that this provision, concerning members of the civil service corps employed in the office serving the Minister of Foreign Affairs in the country, as well as diplomats and national workers employed on the basis of appointment in a foreign institution, is incompatible with the principle of equal treatment regardless of age, which is one of the fundamental principles of Polish labour law. The Legislative Office recalls that according to the article. 11 of the Labour Code any discrimination, including age discrimination, is unacceptable. The Labour Code prohibits age discrimination in Article. 183a §1, according to which workers should be treated equally in relation to the establishment and termination of employment relationships, conditions of employment, promotion and access to training for the improvement of their qualifications. The Legal Office also stresses that the Supreme Administrative Court has expressed itself against the background of the law on bailiffs in a similar case, in that it was an appeal from the position taken by the bailiff, who was 65 years old, precisely in such a spirit that such a principle constitutes a direct discrimination. And the Legislative Office also points out that exceptions to this rule should be included in the law itself, as I said earlier, in order to prevent voluntaryism from being allowed by the Foreign Minister. Finally, the Ombudsman's opinion This is the third opinion I would like to inform the High House of It also crushes both the overall assessment and the detailed assessment of the newly introduced diplomatic secrecy. The Ombudsman considers this to be an important restriction on citizens' access to public information. This restriction on access to public information would also be voluntary. It would not be defined by such principles as all clauses, starting with classified, secret and top secret, in the Act on the protection of classified information. The Ombudsman considers that the citizen has the right to enjoy this access at both national and foreign level, both as regards the functioning of institutions in the country and as regards those representing Poland outside. If, on the other hand, the Ministry of Foreign Affairs wants to keep some information secret, making it proprietary, confidential, top secret, or secret, then there are appropriate tools to do so. Therefore, why impose additional restrictions, he asks the Ombudsman. The rest, ladies and gentlemen... Oh, maybe the High House could use another opinion that shows what it looked like to proceed with this law in the Sejm. Well, the fact that in the Seym we are dealing with haste, we are experiencing in the last five years on the occasion of every bill, and especially these most important laws. The fact that we are dealing with breaches of the principles of legislative technology, the principles described in the relevant regulations, is obvious. We also experience this in this case. But Members also say that the bill was introduced as the first point of the previous sitting of the Sejm without any prejudice. That's one thing. Secondly, the procedure for proceeding in committee was scandalous, where a serious discussion on the shortcomings of the project was prevented and the request for a public hearing was not put to the vote at all. And, after all, not only ambassadors, former ambassadors and lower-level diplomats, but also representatives of the academic community dealing with international or European affairs have much to say on all these issues. Since the current government of university staff recruited many new ambassadors, filling them with such key diplomatic posts as those in Paris or London, why is it that in the case of such a general, fundamental change in the foreign service of the Sejm is not ready to hear their opinion? Thus, the request for a public hearing was not taken into account, nor were the criticisms of the Legislative Office of the Sejm, which were also dismissed. At the same time, he pointed out that the reservations of the Legal Office of the Sejm concerned inter alia. violations of constitutional rules. All these comments were rejected by the Sejm. Ladies and gentlemen, then there were speeches, as I said, committee advisers and committee members. The members of the committee, the senators, will have a chance to speak in the Chamber, and therefore I will not discuss these statements. Maybe I'll just say one thing. Well, according to the majority of foreign affairs committees, this law simply cannot be corrected. Therefore, the adoption of any amendments to this law is not in line with the objective. Unfortunately, this law leads to further destruction of our diplomatic service, and consequently to further lowering the rank of Poland in the world. Thank you very much, Mr. Marshal. So you can say that the Academy of Diplomacy is a road, but the side gate has been opened, from which the Ministry's management You can have such concerns. you will want to make a rich profit. In fact, the head of foreign service is essential in our diplomacy, because he is responsible for the staff. It seems that this long-standing Lenin rule in Poland, that whoever has control of the staff, this one rules, comes back in this law, because it is the head of foreign service who is obliged to report once a year to the Foreign Minister on how staff policy works. Of course, the Minister of Foreign Affairs can always ask the Head of Foreign Services what specific issues, decisions, trends, trends, etc. are like. But you can also imagine the situation if this law does not prohibit that the head of foreign service will say: here you go, next year's information, Minister. This is a pathological situation in which the head of staff becomes a key figure in the department, thanks to his office, thus limiting both the competence of the Foreign Minister and the competence of the Director-General, which makes this vaguely defined triangle of divisive competences in principle possible things that we do not want to see. ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-17-senat-21-1.u34	Thank you very much. Dear Marshal, Ladies and gentlemen of the Senators! High Izbo! I am very pleased that today I have the opportunity to take the floor and present this law to the High House, for which I, and the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, believe so, and so is the position of the Government. We believe that it is extremely important and extremely necessary. I'm glad we can discuss this bill. And, despite these different critical opinions, this position remains unchanged. I would just like to draw attention to something that, unfortunately, is once again manifesting itself in this discussion. We have a law ahead of us in a comprehensive way that changes the key element of the public administration of the foreign service. This element, which is kind of a special category of our civil service. A service which is called upon to represent our country abroad in bilateral relations. The government assumed that after 20 years of validity of the Act in force, this law entered into force on 27 July 2001... no, sorry, it entered into force in 2002 and was adopted in 2001 and a comprehensive reform is needed, some things simply need to be improved. And I am pleased that this kind of work was reflected in the form of a project first, which then After a very long, very substantive discussion in the Sejm, it was supplemented, improved, made several dozen amendments during the committee's work and gained the shape of the bill that we will be discussing today. It is true that the Senate Committee on Foreign Affairs and the European Union has devoted ca. 3 hours, as the President pointed out in his report, but I would like to express some disappointment at the fact that, in fact, Senator Sweed, as he pointed out, if I remember correctly. None of the substantive issues, not one of the substantive amendments, were raised during this meeting. There were a lot of opinions very strongly politically, very emotional, also often indicating the ignorance of matter, to which I will go in a moment. Unfortunately, no elements have been reported that could simply be assessed better or worse and could be discussed whether they are simply improving the functioning of the foreign service or not. I regret it. I'm really sorry about that. Because I believe that the shape of the state administration of the sense of largo, but in general of the Polish state, and in particular the foreign service, is extremely important and we should all care that it works smoothly. If we are not discussing substantively, and there are terms here about some neo-communist standards or the will to destroy foreign service, it is difficult to discuss it differently than going into political polemics. Which, of course, we have the right to do, even we have the obligation, because we are all politicians, and I am also here in a political role. But at the same time, if we are talking about the bill, I think that the substantive issues should be a little more important. Why did we first come out as a ministry on the assumption that such a law was needed? We see that today, at a time when diplomacy is very professional throughout the world, it is necessary to introduce a fundamental distinction, a fundamental division between two aspects of diplomacy. Because, of course, we have this very important aspect. And its importance is growing. Professional diplomacy, the aspect of professional diplomacy, but at the same time we have a strict role a political role which, in principle, is indicated in our constitution by placing the ambassador as head of a foreign office as a body of political nomination. In the current law, this division was unclear and often led to a situation which was not healthy from the point of view of the functioning of the State. This line between professional diplomacy and this factor, which is political, but above all, is democratic, because it represents a government which is the emanation of the democratically elected Sejm and Senate... It is essential that such a clear limit be introduced. And this clear separation of the political factor, the democratic factor, from the official factor, is the essence of this law. That's what's missing so far. We're bringing it in here. And, to be honest, I think that if you look at it from a distance, apart from our current political disputes, from the fact that one or the other formation disagrees with each other, you can see that this law will actually serve every government that will manage the state in the future and will also manage the foreign service in the future. This clear separation causes us to be clear about the situation. But at the same time, separation would not be sufficient. That is why we have introduced amendments, major changes to the foreign service structure. I am referring to the introduction of a new distribution network, a new promotion path in terms of diplomatic degrees, greater transparency in service employment and new solutions in terms of remuneration and social security, social security for foreign service members. We come from the assumption that the foundations of the workings of the foreign service corps must be apoliticality. And that's why we're putting in place a whole list of restrictions that will actually be imposed on professional diplomats in order to ensure this apoliticality. Mr President referred to these limitations too, and I will refer briefly to them in a moment. However, regardless of all of this, we assume that these restrictions will serve professionalization. Because here we rely primarily on how civil service is shaped. Also based on the shape of the Civil Service Act and the existing civil service position of the Head of Civil Service, we are introducing a new position, the position of Head of Foreign Service. This is actually a very similar solution. Because nowhere in any other area of state administration is there such a situation that the Director-General of the Office simultaneously performs the functions for which the Head of Civil Service, responsible for the entire body, is responsible. This situation, which is currently taking place in the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, creates numerous functional problems. And we assume that it is advisable to establish a separate position. And this is what you asked before, Senator, the position of head of foreign service is in fact a position subordinate to the Foreign Minister. And here we are not talking about any emancipation of this position, because it is simply a hierarchical structure. Another issue raised here is the opening of diplomacy. I am aware that you can have different opinions on this, and here we have also been familiar with these opinions. The opinion that at the moment diplomacy is sufficiently open or that it is too closed, sometimes even that it is too open, that it should be closed even more... Such amendments were tabled in the course of work in the Parliamentary Committee by Members of the Platform. We had a debate on this and we assumed that the current mechanism for achieving the diplomatic degree through the diplomatic Academy should be maintained as much as possible. And here, as Senator Szejnfeld pointed out, of course, the Academy of Diplomats, what we have created, this mechanism of professional school for diplomats, a professional school of the highest quality, which educates the elite of Polish diplomacy, is maintained. At the same time, I cannot agree with what Mr Klich said: that there is some side path to the conclusion that the main road is the academy, and we are also introducing a side gate. The diplomatic seminar, which is intended for people already employed in the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, because this is the structure of this path to obtaining a diplomatic degree, will also be conducted by the Academy. This is not a side path. This is a path that at the moment will allow a large group of very good professionals who are employed in the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, but who do not have a diplomatic degree, are often long-term employees, have excellent experience, knowledge of languages, are well aware in various areas, whether territorial or thematic... They will be able, thanks to a diplomatic seminar, without going through a diplomatic application, which, by its very nature, is a career path for younger people, fresh from college... This diplomatic seminar will allow us to introduce into the professional diplomatic service also those people for whom it would be much more difficult at the moment. Here's another thing. Mr. Marshal, I will be brief on the issues that this bill introduces. Here, too, the issue of diplomatic secrecy has been raised. Indeed, it is as the President mentioned, that... and I think it also in some ways contradicts this thesis, that the bill is not absolutely positively judged by anyone. Mr Sikorski, former Minister of Foreign Affairs, whom I believe most of you on my left, clearly said that he does not agree with the criticism of this institution, disagrees with the criticism of the diplomatic secret institution, because he believes that there are such categories of information which are not covered by any classified griffin, any clause that makes it impossible to disclose them, but at the same time their disclosure may be harmful from the State's point of view. This is the kind of information that we are dealing with, and this kind of information falls within the category of diplomatic secrecy. I very rarely have the opportunity to agree with Minister Sikorski on a matter, so I am all the more pleased that today is such an opportunity, because it is a rare situation in which, beyond political divisions, we can agree that a solution is good for you. And I would like us to discuss this bill in such a spirit. Therefore, I will now move on to the issues that were raised as part of the preliminary part, in the course of the questions to the President, because some of these questions were more addressed to the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. Mr President pointed out that the Senate Committee had dealt with this law extremely carefully, that it had been devoted up to three hours to this bill at the Senate Committee meeting. Dear Members, during the meeting of the Parliament's committee, we discussed the law for more than 10 hours and it was discussed in an extremely substantive manner. One rule after the other was discussed, amendments could be tabled to each of these provisions and many amendments were tabled, many amendments were accepted. I think what we started today... Mr Grodzki pointed out that the Sejm accepted one of the Senate's amendments tabled to the Act on amending the scope of reservations to the Istanbul Convention. It is also a bill which I had the honour of presenting in this House together with the Ministry of the Family and the Ministry of Justice. I think that this indicates that if the amendments are substantive in nature, it is really an opportunity to work in substance on this and the Sejm is ready to accept the wise, substantive amendments tabled by the Senate. This is what the Foreign Minister in the Sejm called for. That is also what I also called for during the committee meeting, which unfortunately did not make such amendments, although, as Senator Sweden rightly pointed out, the proposals for such amendments in various letters were in different opinions and we were, and we are, all the time, open to discussion on this issue. I think that if we are talking about the very specific issues that have been raised here, this is the second time that I would like to draw attention to, because this is, I think, extremely important, that it is worth it before criticism, especially one that is not based on an analysis of specific rules, but also on the current rules, and on those which are included in the law. Then we would avoid a situation where we simply make some mistakes, or in a way that is not real, we describe what it looks like. Several symptomatic issues, because unfortunately this phenomenon was evident in the Sejm, during the meeting of the Sejm Committee, as well as during the meeting of the Committee on Foreign Affairs and the European Union in the Senate. The first issue raised by Mr Klich is the alleged ban, which is contrary to the Constitution, on the restriction of trade union membership for professional diplomats. Ladies and gentlemen, the law does not prohibit membership with trade unions. The Act prohibits the performance of professional duties in trade unions. It is an analogy, in principle a word in the word prescribed in the article. 78 (1) (a) (ii) 6 Civil Service Act. This is the professionalization of the official corps, that other types of functions which may be conflicting with the tasks of the service, civil or foreign, should not be performed by people in these positions. It's a professional decision. If someone chooses to be a professional diplomat, they give up certain privileges. But it's not about membership, it's about serving as a trade union, it's about being a very important distinction. Next thing you know, I'm glad Senator Swed noticed. It concerns increasing the scope of social protection, increasing the scope of benefits for our employees, for members of the foreign service. We, when working on this bill, have really spent a lot of time analysing this fact, talking to our employees, seeing what kind of problems arise in their everyday functioning. This applies to both benefits related e.g. with motherhood, with reimbursement of pre-school care fees, with various types of allowances for foreign service, with transitional provisions... in the case of maternity leave, we have introduced the principle that in such a case it may actually be necessary to dismiss workers or employees from the service Because this also applies to paternity leave because the institutions sometimes need a quick replacement, but we keep the foreign allowance paid for 6 months so that this worker is not discriminated against in any way. So that he stays in the service, is not fired from work, or any other consequences do not happen to him. There's a lot of this kind of legislation here. I think it is extremely important that we talk about such specifics, because if we are talking about whether the law makes sense or not, we need to talk about specific regulations, we need to analyse what is at the moment. I thank Senator Rusiecki very much for asking about securing Polish foreign service from the presence of people who were connected with special services, were officers or were secret co-workers of security services and other bodies of totalitarian countries. And here again, I don't want to get very detailed, well, pick on some of the statements, but it must be recalled that it is not the law that forbids such persons to perform this function, because in the act of illustration there are also such records. It is in our Foreign Service Act that we introduce an article. 5, which simply says that such persons cannot be members of the Polish foreign service. We care very much about this. This article in a slightly different version is already in the present law, but we move it to this law and give it a full sound so that it does not actually happen. The issue that raised a lot of objections or questions, it is a question of retirement age for professional diplomats. Here we leave, Dear State, on the assumption that some age limit must be defined, in order for the diplomatic service to naturally adapt to a changing environment. This provision, which we are introducing, is based on general pension regulation, so it applies to people aged 65 years, with this one condition that here, in the case of diplomatic service, we do not make the age difference between men and women. In view of the nature of this work, we have also taken this assumption. But, of course, it is as Senator Komarnicki pointed out, that there may be cases in which people who pass this age still function well, work well, are excellent diplomats. We absolutely want to continue working with these people, so we introduce the principle that any person over the age of 65 will still be able to be a professional diplomat if the Foreign Minister agrees. And here I would like to make a brief reference to the statement made by the President that there is such a principle that something is happening according to the Minister's will. Mr President, we have a system of state so designed that it is the government that is responsible for the functioning of its administration. If the Foreign Minister had no influence on such a key element as the management of the personal condition, the order of the Foreign Service Corps or the Civil Service Corps, it would be a manifestation of some complete dysfunction of the entire system. The Minister of Foreign Affairs from democratic choice, who is the representative of the government appointed by the Sejm, elected in democratic elections, is responsible for his decisions. It is up to the Minister of Foreign Affairs to decide how Polish diplomacy works, otherwise this responsibility will not work. If we deprive the Foreign Minister of influence on such matters, it will in principle not be possible to hold anyone liable in the event that there are reservations about the functioning of the state administration. Ladies and gentlemen, I have briefly referred to the issues that have been raised here, perhaps not to... Excuse me, sir. Senator Czerwinski asked. I've got it written down. The question of citizenship. Mr. Senator, you raised that question about citizenship. I'm in a hurry here too. We introduced the principle that we maintain the obligation to have Polish citizenship by people generally working in foreign service, also for people who are not professional diplomats. As regards diplomats, foreign workers and the head of foreign service, we introduce an exclusive category, which means that these people can only have Polish citizenship. We are aware that this may raise reservations sometimes, but we come from such an assumption that, if someone is a professional diplomat, there should be no possibility that there are suspicions, pressure on such a person for various purposes, even for such a reason that someone may assign him a slightly different intention, because he has a different nationality, even if there is nothing wrong with it. At the same time, however, we are introducing some derogation from this principle for diplomats who will perform this political role, because here this verification mechanism, the control mechanism, the democratic mechanism guarantees this sufficiently. We assume that they should not have the nationality of the country in which they will be in the institution. However, in theory there may be such situations that people with double citizenship perform excellent service for the Republic of Poland in institutions in another country. This is about ambassadors, this is about this political factor, which, as I mentioned, we distinguish from the official factor. That's the idea we came up with. This was the subject of a discussion and a rather long discussion during the session of the Sejm Committee. Dear Senators, I hope that I have briefly addressed all the important issues that have been raised. If any issues remain unclear or you wish to supplement your knowledge, I shall remain open to further questions. I hope that we will discuss here in a substantive manner. I am not running away from any political dispute or dispute about the idea of this law, but I believe that if we question certain concrete solutions, e.g. the appointment of a Head of Foreign Service or the appointment of a Deputy Ambassador for Administrative Affairs, but it is worth pointing out some concrete solutions involved. I hope that this is the nature of the discussion. In the opinion of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, in the opinion of the government, this law in its present form will serve Polish diplomacy well, it will serve the foreign service well, it will serve the Polish state well, anyone who will rule in the future. I therefore recommend that the High House adopt the proposed law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-17-senat-21-1.u42	Thank you very much. Mr. Senator! High Izbo! I answer the first question, which is the question of citizenship. We assumed that in the case of... In fact, different situations are possible. Our employee, diplomat, in some ways also a professional diplomat, in theory can be a citizen of Poland and another country, and yet remain completely, 100% loyal to Poland. However, given that in the case of professional diplomats, the examination procedure is much less detailed, to a much lesser extent based on joint decisions of several key state bodies, it is necessary to introduce here a general principle that no dual citizenship is possible. In the case of ambassadors, we assume that the detail of the procedure to check and the need to make a political decision to present the candidacy to the President... The president then decides to appoint such an ambassador. And yet we are introducing an additional body in the form of a convention, which will also analyse all potential arguments for and against. So that here we can afford to introduce some kind of apostasy. This checking procedure will guarantee our safety. Even if a person has dual citizenship, we will be able to see if he or she is guided by double loyalty. Well, from experience so far, this kind of thing happens. Hence the decision to keep it. As far as domestic workers are concerned, here we have assumed that domestic workers, and therefore workers who are employed on the basis of appointments to perform the duties of support staff members, are persons for whom a slightly milder criteria can be applied than to diplomats. Well, these people, in principle, don't represent you outside, they're just ancillary staff. If we are dealing with any access to classified information, then, of course, these persons undergo a normal examination procedure. If the verification procedure shows that there are doubts, they simply will not be given a security clearance. That's from the Secret Information Protection Act. Therefore, we considered that this additional requirement in the form of exclusive Polish citizenship is not necessary in this case. Although I do realize that you can argue about it. If the practice of applying this law induces us to think about it It's not out of the question. We will re-examine these issues. But it all has to work. Here, Mr. Senator, we assume that because of the different laws in force in different countries, it is impossible to identify one document or one action that a person would have to do. Under the motto of rejuvenation of staff, the employment relationship in the foreign service is to expire on the date on which the person is 65 years old and in the case of the elderly Within 3 months of the entry into force of the Act. In addition, the president will be able to appoint special ambassadors to carry out foreign policy tasks. The diplomat will be able to be transferred to the President's Office, and about whether or not he will, the decision will be made... who? Head of Foreign Affairs at the President's request. The duration of this delegation will not be longer than 5 years, and the diplomat will retain all the existing rights and, after the delegation has ended, the Head of Foreign Services will have to offer such person employment in at least an equivalent position. Does this mean that this is the only place where there's a career opportunity, and that's what the Minister meant? According to the law, you don't need to be a professional diplomat to become head of foreign service. A three-year placement in a managerial position related to international relations will suffice. Even though the Foreign Service Chief has very responsible tasks under the Act. It must ensure compliance with foreign service rules, give diplomatic degrees, indicate criteria for the assessment of foreign service members, arbitraryly direct human resources management process, prepare draft legal acts concerning foreign service, organise training, and, attention, direct the diplomatic Academy and be the only authority empowered to direct candidates to the academy. Its task is also to monitor and supervise the use of funds for the remuneration of members of the Foreign Service corps. As you can see, the head of foreign service has power. What power does the Foreign Minister have? The Head of Foreign Service has also been entrusted with modernising the training system within the framework of the Academy of Diplomat Ministry of Foreign Affairs. It also contains numerous editorial errors, even such as incorrect numbering of its chapters. Is the sloppyness of the bill foretelling greater sloppy conduct of foreign policy? In this situation, the only responsible decision we can make is to reject the bill that was sent to the Senate on the knee. Thank you. [[Applause]]

The introduction of a regulation that says that the employment relationship with a person who is over sixty-five years of age is non-constitutional and incompatible with all principles and documents Also those of an international nature that protect us from discrimination. It goes on, among other things. o Council Directive 2000/78/EC of 27 November 2000 establishing a general framework for equal treatment in employment and occupation. Our Supreme Court has also repeatedly expressed its views on this subject, pointing out that the introduction of laws that automatically eliminate the employment relationship of particular groups of workers is a discriminatory measure for these groups of employees. In doing so, the legislator celebrates both the provisions of the Labour Code The ban on discrimination on grounds of age As well as the constitutional provisions. In its judgment of 26 September 2019, the Supreme Court stated that legislative action in this respect is unworthy of Article. 24, 30, 32 and 60 of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. I would like to take this away from the fact that the employee will be able to obtain the Minister's permission to continue serving. Of course, it will be an arbitrary, uncomplainable decision. The Act does not provide any criteria on the basis of which such consent is to be expressed. It is not a question of psychophysical predisposition, experience, fitness to work on a given job. It is to be expected that, as has been the case for all decisions made in recent years, these decisions will be made on the basis of a political criterion. And that's unacceptable. Harmful not only for Polish diplomatic service and Polish diplomacy I don't know what else could hurt her after your government. But it's also harmful to the state. Thank you very much.

Here it is worth recalling that this valorization is the average annual rate of price and service increases, i.e. inflation, plus a minimum of 20% real increase in average salary in the previous calendar year. So we're talking about 2019, if we're talking about 2020 and if we're talking about 2021, we're talking about 2020. Then, in September, we accepted this indicator at level 3.84, those were the forecasts we estimated. That is why we have proposed a solution that we have already adopted, i.e. quota-percent valorisation, so as to secure the lowest benefit at the appropriate level. In this case we secured this at the level of 50 PLN, i.e. the lowest benefits would increase by 50 PLN, i.e. out of the current amount of PLN 1 thousand 200 would increase to PLN 1 thousand 250. At the same time, we have put in place a mechanism that safeguards that, in the case of higher inflation, which is always announced at the beginning of February of a given year, in this case this year... If it turns out that inflation is higher or the wage rate is higher, then we have this fuse in order to be able to apply not the amount value, but the percentage. And so in this case it happened that the declared inflation rate... We proceed with the examination of the fourth item of the agenda: the Act amending the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments and certain other laws. The text of the Act is contained in print No 312 and the report of the Commission In print No 312 A. I ask the rapporteur of the Committee on Budgets and Public Finance, Senator Kazimierz Kleina, to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-17-senat-21-1.u179	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Committee on Budget and Public Finance a report on the work of our committee on the Act amending the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments and some other laws. Our report is in print No 312 A. The Commission recommends that this law be adopted without amendment. The Committee on Budgets and Public Finances considered this law on 10 February this year. It was adopted on 21 January 2021. As I said, we recommend the adoption of a law without amendment. I will briefly discuss this bill, present its assumptions. The bill is very complicated and very hermetic. It concerns quite important, important financial elements, but it is to a large extent a technical law. The basic objective of the Act is to align the Polish legal order with EU legislation in connection with the entry into force of the European Union legal acts, which are the result of a review of the provisions concerning European supervisory authorities. The legislation in question implements the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council, i.e. Directive 2177 of 2019 amending the previous Directive of 2009, No 138/ The EC relating to the taking up and pursuit of the business of insurance and reinsurance and the 2014 Directive No 65 on markets in financial instruments, as well as the 2015 Directive on the prevention of the use of the financial system for money laundering or terrorist financing and complements the implementation of the 2015 Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council on payment services in the internal market, amending the Directives of 2002, 2009 and 2013 and the 2010 Regulation of the European Union No 1093 on the establishment of a European Supervisory Authority, Regulation No 1094 of the Union on the establishment of a European Supervisory Authority... These are regulations that govern the functioning of this European Supervisory Authority. These regulations are undergoing quite significant changes, which requires further changes and implementation of these provisions into Polish law. This law also requires the amendment of the Act of 29 July 2005 on trading in financial instruments, the Act of 29 July 2005 on the supervision of the capital market and other laws which relate to payment services or the introduction of financial instruments into organised trading and public companies. This law also amends the Commercial Companies Code Act and some other laws. The Union legislature has amended the rules on the activities of providers of information provision services which provide services as an approved publication entity, an approved reporting mechanism or a consolidated information provider. A solution has been introduced to transfer the authorisation and supervision of information-sharing services providers from the level of the competent national authorities to the European Securities and Markets Authority. The exception will be approved publication entities or approved reporting mechanisms provided that they meet the identification criteria set out in the delegated act issued by the European Commission referred to in the 2014 Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council, i.e. Document No 600 of 15 May 2014 A change has also been made to improve consumer protection. To this end, provision has been made for payment service providers to make available a brochure drawn up by the European Commission containing information to consumers about their rights. The document I am talking about has introduced a number of solutions that improve the situation of consumers in relation to the various financial institutions. The amendment of the Act of 29 July 2005 on Trading in Financial Instruments and the Act of 29 July 2005 on Supervision of the Capital Market will result in the elimination from the Polish legal order of provisions concerning operational requirements for providers of information provision services and the powers of the Financial Supervision Commission with regard to providers of such services. The purpose of the amendments is also to enable the KNF to carry out supervision and any activities related to it in connection with the supervision of approved publication entities or the approved reporting mechanisms referred to in delegated acts adopted by the European Commission on the basis of the relevant provisions. With regard to the Law of 29 July 2005 on the supervision of the capital market, the Act also provides for the possibility for the European Securities and Markets Authority to delegate supervisory tasks to KNF which have so far been in competence... The 2011 amendment to the Payment Services Act will result in the obligation for payment service providers to make available to the consumer free of charge the information booklet of the European Commission on consumer rights when making payments in Europe. As you senators remember, the issue of payment services, payment cards and all the things that concern non-cash trading, we in the Senate were already regulated in 2011. So now these rules are being amended and we are also introducing rules that protect consumers more on the basis of European legislation. The amendment of the Act of 11 September 2015 on Insurance and Reinsurance Activities provides for the obligation on the KNF to promptly communicate to the competent supervisory authorities of other Member States of the European Union and to the European Insurance and Occupational Pensions Authority information on national insurance undertakings or national reinsurance undertakings which carry out or intend to carry out significant cross-border activities within their territory and on the risks associated with those activities. In addition, KNF will be required to inform the competent supervisory authorities of other Member States of the European Union immediately about the cross-border activities of foreign insurance companies in the territory of the Republic of Poland. Regardless of the problems and issues I have mentioned, the legislator is making changes in the functioning of public companies. The changes involve dematerialisation of the shares, the departure from the paper form of the share document. With effect from 1 March 2021, shares shall only operate in the form of a security account or in a securities deposit. These changes are of a sequencing nature. And in connection with Brexit This is the last group of changes. It is necessary to regulate in the Act the matters relating to the performance of insurance contracts, reinsurance contracts and other contracts concluded by insurance and reinsurance undertakings established in the United Kingdom of Great Britain, Northern Ireland and Gibraltar on 31 December 2020 until the expiry of claims from those contracts, which were established on a uniform licence basis in Poland. Such agreements shall in principle remain valid. The withdrawal of the United Kingdom from the European Union shall not constitute grounds for termination of the contract or affect the obligation to fulfil the contractual benefit. In general, the Act shall enter into force on 1 January 2022, except that it does not apply to all provisions. The Brexit provision will enter into force on a retroactive basis as from 1 January 2021. Amendment of the Payment Services Act 14 days after the publication of the Act. The amendments to the Act on Public Offering and the Conditions of Introduction of Financial Instruments to Organized Trading and Public Companies and the Act of 2019 amending the Act. The Code of Commercial Companies will enter into force on 1 March 2021. The provisions on Insurance and Insurance Activities will enter into force on 30 June 2021. And that would be the most important elements of the bill. I would also like to inform the High House that at a committee meeting with the Minister we discussed the provisions of this law. The Legislative Office has tabled one proposal for an amendment, but it is legislative in nature. The Commission considered that this amendment was not relevant and that extending the date of entry into force of the law would create more risks than those resulting from this legislative failure. We therefore accepted the proposal that this law should be adopted without amendments. During our discussions, and those at the committee meeting, and those in preparation for the committee meeting, there was a problem with the provisions of the Act. And here would also be a question to the Minister, asking for a possible explanation of this problem, concerning the trading of shares in companies that are bankrupt or in liquidation. Because the problem that exists today is that these shares are often traded, especially in the case of owners or minority shareholders, even though the company is in liquidation or bankruptcy. In order to prevent, in the future, such situations, that there may be transactions that may make those buyers feel deceived, in short, worthless shares The law should not, perhaps not now, but on the occasion of the next amendment, make a proper change and prevent such situations? I'd like to ask the Minister for some explanation, a comment on the situation. So much for my report. Thank you very much for your attention.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-17-senat-21-1.u184	Dear Marshal! High Senate! The proposed law aims to implement the European Union's legislation on European supervisory authorities. These changes mainly concern the capital and insurance sectors. As regards the capital market, rules on the supervision of providers of capital market information services are being revised. In principle, the Act transfers supervisory powers over providers of such services from the level of the national supervisory authority, which is the Commission for Financial Supervision, to the European level, to the European Securities and Markets Authority, i.e. ESMA. It should be pointed out that at least three categories of service providers can be distinguished in the European financial market: an approved publication entity, an approved reporting mechanism and a consolidated information provider. I said theoretically, because this third form in Europe does not yet exist in any country, it is a legal concept, but in practice no one has ever founded such an institution. And here maybe I could answer the question that you asked Senator Swed if there would be a transfer from national supervision to ESMA that this would depend on how large and significant the activities of the institution in terms of providing information will be. If this importance is not systemic, then, under the guidelines in the European Commission Delegated Regulation, such an institution may be subject to national supervision. And here I explain that currently in Poland only one entity, namely the National Securities Deposit SA, is authorised to act as an approved reporting mechanism. As regards the insurance sector, the solution concerns the issue of improving international cooperation. The Act obliges the Financial Supervision Commission to communicate certain information to the competent supervisory authorities of other Member States and to the European Insurance and Occupational Pensions Authority, the Authority. This information will apply to national insurance undertakings or national reinsurance undertakings which carry out or intend to carry out significant cross-border activities within their territory. In addition, it will be possible to improve the exchange of information through new tools, namely cooperation platforms. The Act also contains solutions to improve consumer protection, as President Kazimierz Kleina has already said. A regulation has been proposed to allow, inter alia, the performance of insurance contracts concluded until 31 December 2020 by insurance undertakings established in the United Kingdom. In terms of improving consumer protection, provision is also proposed for the provision by payment service providers of a brochure drawn up by the European Commission containing information to consumers about their rights. The Act also provides for clarification of the provisions relating to the application of those regulations in the field of public companies which are required to announce a call for action when the company is withdrawn from trading on the Stock Exchange or from the NewConnect market. This is also a protection measure for minority investors, i.e. de facto it can be said that to a large extent pro-consumer. As regards the discussion at the meeting of the Committee on Budgets and Public Finance, I recall that there was such a request to refer to the comments of the President of the Office for Personal Data Protection. I think that these comments... maybe not attention, but the doubts have been explained in writing, but I will still allow myself to quote them. The President's first comment was on the request to clarify the wording of the information. I would like to point out that this concept is defined directly in Council and Parliament Regulation No 600/2014, and this cannot be changed. The President's second and third comments raised doubts as to whether the transmission of information to supervisory authorities by providers of information-sharing services and by insurance companies would concern personal data. Here I would like to say that this will not apply to such data. That'll be, for example. information in the framework of the exchange of cooperation on the platform between insurance supervision and concern This will be the issue of the risk profile of the plant offered by the plant in the context of cross-border activities of products, rates or any other financial data, e.g. procedures for determining technical provisions or distribution channels in insurance undertakings. There was also a question about the issue of trading in shares of companies in liquidation or bankruptcy. As far as regulated markets are concerned, it is regulated by stock rules. As a general rule, companies in bankruptcy or liquidation cannot be admitted to this market, but if they are already on the market, they should no longer, in fact, participate in it as regards trading in shares. Nevertheless, there are some possibilities... If it would be appropriate for the company to still be listed, then, as I recall, it is possible. But then it is public information that the company is already in liquidation or bankruptcy. Nevertheless, with regard to the OTC market, i.e. the private market, I would say that this already goes beyond the scope of the Act on Trading in Financed Instruments, which mainly relates to the market, can be said to be regulated, public. Nevertheless, I think the amendments to the Commercial Companies Code, which we are referring to here, which concern the dematerialisation of trading in securities, may be such a summary to, so to speak, limiting the risk of negative consequences of trading on the private market. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-17-senat-21-1.u226	Thank you very much, Mr. Marshal. High Izbo! The four main issues are related to the government bill we are discussing today. The first issue is to give priority to a pedestrian who enters the pedestrian crossing. Today, pedestrians are given priority on the pedestrian crossing, and we extend this priority and include pedestrians who will enter the pedestrian crossing. Secondly, we prohibit the use of phones when passing through the road, we prohibit the use of phones and other electronic devices that distract perception, distract attention and which may have bad consequences when passing through the road. Thirdly, we harmonise the permitted speed in the built-up area. Today, this maximum permitted speed between 23.00 and 5.00 a.m. is up to 60 km/h, and after 5.00 a.m. to 23.00 is 50 km/h. We standardize this speed to 50 km/h. These are practices in other European countries, these are the rules in countries west of Poland, and I think it is also worth introducing such a provision with us. Here I immediately answer one of the questions that came up in the discussion, and yesterday it was also asked at the meeting of the Senate Committee on Infrastructure. Well, in 2019 there were eighty. A few pedestrian victims from between 11:00 and 5:00 a.m. Therefore, it is also worth interfering and calming traffic during the night, even though there is less intensity, which is understandable. And the fourth issue The one which, according to the discussion heard, raises the most... well, maybe not the doubts, but the most questions about the detail of this issue. It's a so-called ban. Bumper ride. It is worth noting that this ban will apply on high-speed roads, i.e. motorways and expressways, and it will also be expressed in meters... The speed that the driver will travel, we divide by 2, from which we will have a minimum distance that we will be obliged to keep from the vehicle that will move ahead. So if we drive at a speed of 120 km/h, the minimum distance from the vehicle ahead is 60 m. So that is the case with the proposals that have been prepared by the government. Some of these proposals were announced by Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki in his exposé, and today we come to implement them. I would also like to stress very strongly and say this with full responsibility that I would not be here, we would not propose these regulations, if not for other systemic changes which we want to make when it comes to building safety on Polish roads. The first systemic change is defined by the "Safe infrastructure' programme, which will be adopted in the form of a resolution at the next meeting of the Council of Ministers, most likely next Tuesday. In principle, we already have PLN 2.5 billion budgeted for this programme and within 4 years, in the period 2021.2024, these funds will be used for spot investments in national road networks such as the lighting of pedestrian crossings, improving the environment of pedestrian crossings, construction of pavements, walking-bike lines, safe crossings, whether with light signaling, whether in the form of roundabouts, building asylums where possible, building coves where necessary. So that it will be a point investment in national road networks over the next four years. Of course, these investments have been carried out and have been carried out over the last years, but now we will be making more of these investments in countries motivated by these regulations. Another important infrastructure element, investment, is that the same investments will be carried out by local governments, municipalities and powiats within the additional financial envelope that was secured in 2020. This month, the Voivodeships will announce recruitment for projects from the Government Road Development Fund, where we will allocate over PLN 500 million just for investments consisting of, among others. on improvement of pedestrian transitions. Lighting in an appropriate way, marking or construction of another infrastructure, which affects the safety of pedestrian crossings, all of which can be financed from the Government Road Development Fund. And very quickly, because, as I said, this month, the voivods will announce this recruitment. I will mention very briefly the national road construction programme, where the main importance was given to the construction of high-speed roads. I will also mention our 100 bypass programme, which will also affect road safety. Circumventions of cities and towns with sometimes compact and very narrow buildings are also important from the point of view of road safety. So that this investment sphere is being conducted, but thanks to the decisions of the Government of Law and Justice, which have been taken so de facto in the previous year, it will be increased. Another very important issue that we discussed in the Seym and in the Senate on the occasion of this project is an educational campaign. Yes, the educational campaign in connection with these regulations must be very large, it must reach anyone who uses Polish road infrastructure every day, and in fact we all use it, whether driving a vehicle, whether walking or cycling. We have already prepared advertising spots, they at the right time, when these regulations will enter into force, and according to the record they are to enter into force on 1 June this year... Then this campaign will be launched so that this public awareness of the correct and correct attitudes of both pedestrians and drivers is mass. As part of this educational campaign, we want to reach out to every participant in the road, and basically to every Pole and every Pole. We have funds for this, more than PLN 30 million, of which 28 million are funds from the Operational Programme of Infrastructure and the environment. These spots are actually filmed, and we're waiting, colloquially speaking, to launch them when the time of entry into force of these laws is approaching. And another element, which is also ahead of us, but this may be a further future, calculated in months or quarters, is also the strengthening of executions. Many of you also draw attention to the need for a speeding system to work more efficiently and that anyone who commits a speeding offence faster than the speed limit be punished. We're working on a system that's gonna improve the downloadability of speed cameras. So that these changes, when it comes to strengthening executions, are also ahead of us. Therefore, I should say yes, by the end of my introduction, consider this law part of a system solution that is related to improvements in road traffic. Of course, we will monitor its effects, we will monitor its effects, and if there is a need for corrections, we are not blocked either. We will not insist if any solutions need to be improved or changed. We firmly believe that, thanks to our comprehensive actions, also through this law, road safety will be increased. Senate Committee on National Defence 11 February this year worked on a law which is contained in Senate printing No 311 as well as in Seym printing No 764. We are dealing with a government submission which was adopted by the Sejm on 21 January 2021. The Act amending the Act on the Protection of Persons and Property and the Act on the Military Gendarmery and Military Ordering Authorities aims to regulate the detailed arrangements for the supervision of specialized armed protective formations, in short called SUFOs, which operate in areas, facilities and equipment of organisational units subordinate to or supervised by the Minister of National Defence. The amendment provides for the supervision of these formations to be exercised by the Minister of National Defence through the Chief Commandant of the Military Gendarmerie. The supervision will concern the organisation, operating rules, weapons and equipment, cooperation with other formations and services, as well as the compliance of the current security status of the unit with the plans. The Military Gendarmerie will have the right to enter the protected areas and facilities, request explanations, access to protective documentation, entry to the premises of the operator carrying out the protective activities and to make written recommendations to remedy the deficiencies found, and adapt the security company's activities to the legal provisions. Surveillance activities will also be able to be recorded. The Act also provides that the Commandant of the Military Police will report to the Minister of National Defence. These are the main objectives of this bill. It is worth noting that in the Act we are talking about supervision, which is exercised by the Minister of National Defence, but this supervision is also exercised by the Chief Police Officer The area under the responsibility of the Minister for the Interior is concerned. I would like to mention here the question that you asked Vice-President Jerzy Wcisła during the discussion: why only to a certain extent The Minister for National Defence Is this amendment going to happen? As Minister Skurkiewicz, present at the meeting of the committee and also present at the Senate, has explained, there have been no previous amendments proposed by the Minister for the Interior or the Chief Commissioner in this area. Senator Bogdan Zdrojewski also spoke in the discussion, who informed that the work in this area had been expected much earlier and even started. It is about the work on this supervision by the Minister for National Defence. Yeah, but they're finding it right now, I hope, their finale in this government proposal. Minister Zdrojewski also asked a question during the committee's deliberations: will the military police's personal condition increase because of this? The personal state of the Military Gendarmerie as a specialized ordering service of the Polish Armed Forces in this area will not change and will be sufficient to carry out tasks. During the discussion, Senator Jerzy Czerwiński also asked the question: in the context of these changes will it be necessary to amend the rules on the protection and state of planning of units? From the opinion of both Minister and Counselor Mandylis of the Legislative Bureau, there was no need for this. Besides, the legal opinion was very clear. The law without amendments in this respect is very much expected, as also evidenced by the opinions attached to the Act, submitted by the association of security workers, as well as the association of security workers, which share the opinion of the Minister for National Defence on the subject of the amendments made. The bill received a positive opinion from the National Defence Committee, with 7 senators in favour, no one opposed, and two senators abstained. It was, Mr. Marshal, in a concise way, to convey what was at the committee meeting. Thank you very much.

The draft law is contained in print No 271 and the report is published in print No 271 S. Four amendments were tabled during the committee's deliberations. One of these amendments seeks to correct the staff capital plans within the time limits and is the result of a shift of proceedings on this draft law for the period after 1 January, and this self-correction was therefore necessary. This self-correction was accepted by the committee in the vote: 25 votes in favour, 4 against, 11 senators abstained. The second amendment concerns this provision, which refers to sickness benefits. In the meantime, the rules have changed and the isolation institution has been introduced into the law. This amendment was adopted: 22 votes in favour, 0 against, 19 senators abstained. The third amendment also refers to the rules on PPKs. In this case, those entities which have been exempted from the provisions within the time limit are excluded. They've done their duty. And the last, fourth amendment is a legislative, formal addition to the provision confirming that this law is in its scope compatible with European Union rules on State aid. The whole draft law was adopted by 24 votes to 16 against and 2 senators abstained and thus received an overwhelming majority of the members of the committee. So much for the committee's work report. Let me just remind you in a nutshell, because those interested in knowledge, those who took part in the first reading in committees, which is the bill. Let me remind you that it refers to employee capital plans. In this respect, it is proposed to postpone the deadlines for both entrepreneurs and all obliged entities, including those representing the public finance sector, by one year in relation to the pandemic. Thus, this applies to some of the entities that carried it out in the second stage, the third stage, and all those in the fourth stage. The projecter was particularly concerned with the support of micro-entrepreneurs and small companies employing up to 20 employees. These provisions also include a proposal for abolition, i.e. not punishing those companies who have failed to fulfil these obligations within the deadlines set so far. Another proposal is a proposal that concerns companies that do not employ employees. The aim is to include these companies in the state aid scheme provided during the pandemic. I'm afraid so. The designers do not know what the reason is, and Polish entrepreneurs look at it with astonishment That, in principle, the entire system of aid created by you to entrepreneurs, no matter how we judge it at this point, ignores it to its essential extent... It goes on for example. about the State Development Fund. It ignores companies that employ a lot of employees very often, but they operate in specific industries. Well, enough to mention remote trade or insurance. Surely, a number of these companies are tourist companies, where people are employed not on the basis of employment relationships, only as part of self-employment or under agency agreement, contract-ordering, contract for work. These companies, e.g. Tourist offices, otherwise, could not function at all. It's the same with insurance companies doing their business through agencies and agents. These are companies that often have dozens and even hundreds of employees, but with different legal status. For this reason, they were only formally excluded from the possibility of obtaining aid from the State Development Fund, as well as from many other support instruments. It can't stay anyway, because it's hurting these companies. A bunch of them. I've already mentioned here, for example, tourist companies, belong to those industries, which completely, totally lie. Well, you can say so colloquially when it comes to the effects of the economic crisis. And these companies can't get help. Another proposal from this project is to cover the sickness benefit of those workers who, due to illness, isolation, isolation or quarantine associated with the pandemic, cannot carry out their work within the framework of the employment relationship. On the one hand, the aim is to ensure that these workers do not have a mental barrier, and especially a financial barrier, and that they have it, provide information practically from all over Poland. So that they do not hide their health or the fact that they are infected so that they do not try to heal themselves in fear that if they report to their employer or to a doctor with symptoms associated with COVID-19, they will be sent either for treatment or for quarantine or isolation. It all ends with a financial loss for such an employee. That's why there's a proposal here that, in this particular situation, in a pandemic period, workers can receive... and actually they can't receive, just get 100%. sickness benefit instead of 70% . The point is, there's no barrier here. As I said, she is not only psychological, but above all financial, which prompts you to hide your health. On the other hand, at a time when a pandemic is going on, employers have huge financial problems and should not bear any costs. So we propose that from day one Not since the third thirty, as it is now in the law The sickness benefit was paid by the Social Insurance Institution, not by the employer. Another proposal in this project concerns an instrument that would support the maintenance of the financial liquidity of companies during a pandemic, not outside it, not when the pandemic is over. Let's hope it happens as soon as possible. This is to reduce the VAT refund period from 60 to 14 days. This is a virtually non-stop repeated postulate of organizations representing entrepreneurs and employers, repeated from the beginning, from the outbreak of a pandemic to now. What is most difficult for companies that are faced with problems related to the effects of the pandemic is maintaining liquidity, fulfilling private and public obligations without borrowing, without borrowing, etc. Companies very often, waiting for their own money, those that belong to them, but are on the accounts of the tax office, because there are 60 days for their return, have to save themselves with a turnover loan to maintain liquidity. Therefore, there is a proposal to shorten this deadline from 60 to 14 days. The last proposal contained in this law is a proposal to include members of single-member companies with limited liability and open-ended companies, i.e. partnerships, limited partnership companies, among those benefiting from the aid. Until now, the law has not treated these partners in this way, and these entities, these companies and their partners could not benefit from the aid, e.g. granted in a parking form. After the entry into force of these provisions, a member of a single-member limited liability company or partner company will be treated as an employee. If there are specific reasons and the criteria for applying for a parking grant can be applied, then such a company will be able to benefit from it. There is no such law today. So much for information on the proceedings of the joint committees and a reminder of the ideas and objectives of the bill. Thank you very much, Mr. Marshal. Thank you, Mrs. Marshal. Current data indicate that more and more people are dying of COVID due to a late appointment with doctors.
